ADDING THE TACTICAL ASSESSMENT

     D-28. Once the evaluator completes the tactical task training and evaluation outlines for each step’s
     corresponding tasks, the evaluator places the result in the far-right column of the targets killed by step format.
     Figure D-7 shows all the training and evaluation outline assessments adjudicated and placed on the score
     sheet.

     Figure D-7. Adding the tactical assessment ratings (targets killed by step format)

                                                                                                                         Collective Evaluation

DETERMINING OVERALL PROFICIENCY

     D-29. The evaluator verifies the overall proficiency is adjudicated correctly using their score sheet. Table VI,
     LFX proficiency is earned by the unit that receives:

          • A GO on 70 percent of the live-fire tasks overall.
          • Greater than 70-percent “T” or “P” assessments on all live-fire tasks within the day and night

                scenario.

     D-30. Figure D-8 shows the completed day phase from the previous targets killed by step format examples
     and the results of the night phase of the training event. Those results combined equate to the live-fire
     proficiency rating.

              Figure D-8. Overall proficiency rating (targets killed by step format)

D-31. As shown in figure D-8, even with the night live-fire kill percentage is an overall NO-GO, the training
unit holistically is rated proficient with both the day and night kill standard and the day and night tactical
assessments are both greater than 70 percent.

25 April 2025  TC 3-20.20  273

                                                Appendix E

                Collective Tasks by Formation Type

    This appendix provides a list of platoon collective tasks and unit task lists, broken down by
    each of the sixteen maneuver formation types found within the three BCTs: ABCT, IBCT, and
    SBCT. Commanders, platoon leaders, platoon sergeants, and subordinate leaders can use this
    appendix as a guide to build training plans for their individual Soldiers and small units prior to
    conducting collective training. The figures listed in this appendix will provide leaders with a
    quick reference guide for collective tasks on which their unit may be evaluated.

COLLECTIVE TASK REQUIREMENTS

     E-1. The platoon training strategy requires organizations to perform certain tasks for evaluation using either
     Table IV, FTX or Table VI, LFX. These tasks are required to achieve unit proficiency.

     E-2. The required tasks are derived from the following:
          • Mission-essential task.
          • Supporting collective task—five to seven tasks listed as critical, collective tasks as part of the
                mission-essential task.
          • Decisive action enabling task.
          • Basic tactical task.
          • Integrated warfighting function tasks—integrated warfighting function tasks required to show
                proficiency at a specific capability, function, or action.
          • Operational environment—four or more operational environment conditions must be included in
                the scenario.
          • Commander selected—determined by commanders based on their current unit assessment or
                command training guidance.

     E-3. Figures E-1 through E-16, pages 276 through 283 outline examples of the collective tasks that each
     unit type will be required to conduct. These tasks are listed by individual table of organization and equipment
     code. Leaders can use these unique codes, as well as their specific unit identification code, to obtain the
     proper mission-essential task list, full unit task list, and training and evaluation outlines by utilizing the
     following resources:

          • Army Training Network.
          • Central Army Registry.
          • CATS.
          • DTMS.

25 April 2025  TC 3-20.20  275

are marked with an asterisk. The proponent manual for other terms is listed in parentheses after
the definition.

                                                                                                                                          Glossary

  SECTION II – TERMS

company
          A unit consisting of two or more platoons, usually of the same type, with a headquarters and a limited
          capacity for self-support. (ADP 3-90)

platoon
          A subdivision of a company or troop consisting of two or more squads or sections. (ADP 3-90)

section
          (Army) A tactical unit of the Army and Marine Corps smaller than a platoon and larger than a squad.
          (ADP 3-90)

squad
          A small miliary unit typically containing two or more fire teams. (ADP 3-90)

troop
          A company-size unit in a cavalry organization. (ADP 3-90)

warfighting function
          A group of tasks and systems united by a common purpose that commanders use to accomplish
          missions and training objectives. (ADP 3-0)
